# Tech debt tracker

Real but non-blocking findings that were deliberately not fixed when found. Each entry names the owner child, the evidence, and what would close it. Items are removed when closed, with the closing commit noted in the charter's Decision Log if the closure changed a contract.

## Schedule (owner's ruling 2026-09-28: debt is fixed in waves, not accumulated)

After each merge the architect triages every row into one of three bins. A row never sits in the table without one.

- **Wave 1**, dispatched right after C5 merged as two workers in isolated worktrees off master, one commit per row, `pnpm verify`, bounded review on opus, fix waves, merge. Engine-host half landed at a164694 (six rows closed: the readSpill oracle in three rounds, since the review kept finding a stronger variant, ending with a refusal of any non-normal path; the queue release, with a `refused` drop pausing only when messages wait; host-minted cancels; `memoryAppend` folders; `INTERRUPT_MARKER`; the initial `starting` status). Main-and-shell half in its fix loop (five Minors from review, all being fixed rather than logged). Original list: the `App.test.tsx` flake; the `panel.<appSessionId>` key on `session.removed`; the 960 px clamp; the `will-quit` order; raw `<command-name>` titles; the `fs.readSpill` not-found oracle; the dropped-before-`started` queue release and its wording (before C7 builds on the queue); the replay transport's host-minted cancels; `session.memoryAppend` creating the folder; `INTERRUPT_MARKER` exported from C3; the git watcher's bounded items (counts tied to the requesting window, untracked directories); `SessionRecord.gateway.routed` as `boolean | null` (X1 amended: null is "unknown", the state of every discovered session; C11 reads it); C3 emitting the initial `starting` status before a launch's first rows (C5's second re-check, Minor).

| 2026-10-10 | E13 follow-up (socket peer authentication, cua board #95, MAWS PR #7; amends the row of 2026-10-08, E13, "Security note: the cookie-jar…") | The 2026-10-08 E13 row's cookie reads stand, but who reaches them changed. Since 2026-10-09 (cua spec `2026-10-09-maws-socket-peer-auth-design.md`): the session's cua socket accepts only a peer that runs as MAWS's user and descends from that session's engine process instance (pid and start time, re-verified at every check), refuses any other before reading a byte, and refuses every peer when its addon is missing; cua's relay inside `cua serve` (plugin 0.7.0) accepts only descendants of that `cua serve` process. Other applications, other sessions and Terminal clients are cut off. The residual, accepted by the spec's owner: a peer is accepted by where it sits in the process tree, not by what it is. Every engine descendant is admitted (the model's Bash commands among them), bounded only by the rules every peer meets (the cookie-jar refusals, internal tabs out of reach, the takeover hold); and because the engine starts with `--setting-sources user,project,local` and without `--strict-mcp-config` (`src/engine-host/launch.ts`), a same-user process that writes a hook or an MCP server into `~/.claude/settings.json` or a project's `.claude/settings.local.json`, or edits the user-writable plugin files, gets its own code started as an engine descendant. Two same-user routes need no configuration write. (a) SIGUSR1: every stock Node process in an admitted tree (the engine's other Node MCP servers, Node processes the model starts, the vendor runtime's Node launcher `cua_node/bin/node`, measured to open an inspector on 127.0.0.1:9229) answers a same-user `kill -USR1` with an inspector that runs the sender's code as a descendant; only cua's own processes (`cua serve`, the anchor) start with `--disable-sigusr1`. MAWS's main process and the detached supervisor (the app binary run as Node) answered it too (measured 2026-10-09), where code needs no socket at all; closed for both on 2026-10-10: a no-op `process.on('SIGUSR1', () => {})` listener at their entries, which keeps Playwright's `--inspect=0` working, and for the supervisor `--disable-sigusr1` at its spawn (`src/main/hosts/detached-transport.ts`), so it is closed from exec on. Main cannot take that flag, so a short startup window remains in main: Node's SIGUSR1 handler interrupts synchronous code, and a signal that lands before the entry's listener line runs (after the module bodies imported ahead of it evaluate) still opens an inspector. The app's fuses stay a MAWS-wide decision: `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments` (Enabled; the harness's `--inspect=0` needs it), `EnableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` (Enabled) and `OnlyLoadAppFromAsar` (Disabled) leave same-user routes into the app for whoever controls its launch environment or its bundle. (b) The supervisor's 0600 token file (`src/engine-host/detached.ts`, `writeToken`) is readable by any same-user process, which can then take over the supervisor and the session's real engine. So the check stops a same-user process that neither modifies what the engine executes nor takes over the processes that already have access: other applications and casual or drive-by clients, not an attacker who can rewrite the owner's Claude Code configuration or read MAWS's own token files. | The check in `src/main/browser/cua/peer.ts` and `index.ts`; the listeners in `src/main/index.ts` and `src/engine-host/detached.ts` and the supervisor's spawn flag, checked by `e2e/sigusr1.spec.ts` and `e2e/supervisor-packaged.spec.ts`; P1 A-51 and A-52 (amended 2026-10-09); the peer authentication spec's Purpose, Surprises (M2's and M3's SIGUSR1 measurements) and Decision Log. | Revisit if the supervisor's token route is closed by a check of its own (a MAWS-wide decision); the engine launch is hardened with `--strict-mcp-config` or narrower setting sources (a separate charter decision: §4.1's launch line, and the charter's rule that settings, hooks, MCP servers and plugins load as they do in the terminal); the vendor runtime ever starts its Node with `--disable-sigusr1` (cua's `launch.mjs` strips `NODE_OPTIONS`, and the runtime stays unmodified); or the app's fuses above are decided (a MAWS-wide decision). The cookie reads stay while the vendor needs those events. Owner: E13 follow-up. |

| 2026-10-19 | DC3 (the desk hub, `apps/desk-hub/src/objects/registry.ts` `pushDirectory`; `project.ts` `reportFailed`), narrowed 2026-10-21 (`desk-org-retry`) | The org directory's two hops, the Registry's push of each change to every `Machine` and a `Project`'s summary report that carries a lease's lapse or return, are sent again after a failure (`retry.ts`: up to eight sends over one to two minutes, a later change or report superseding the retry), but the retry waits in its sender's memory. A failed hop whose sender is itself reset or replaced before its retry goes is lost as before, until the next change of the directory or of that project, or that machine's next stream: a deploy that reaches both ends of a failed call within the call. A `Project` replaced so cannot clear its dedupe key (`summary`) either, so its successor withholds the same counts until they change. The Registry's half is the narrower: a deploy replaces every `Machine` too, whose next stream opens with the directory. | The review of `desk-org-push` (MAWS #66). `desk-org-retry`'s suites inject the failures into the calls; Miniflare cannot replace an object under a call it is making. | A hop's retry outlives its sender's reset: what the hop still owes kept durably (the report's acknowledged copy and an alarm, say), a storage change for the owner's ruling first. |

| 2026-10-23 | MAWS #129 (desk transcript free scroll) | `TranscriptList`'s re-follow accepts the end it last observed (`known.end` in `readScroll`, Transcript.tsx), so that a return to the bottom whose scroll event arrives after the extent grew still resumes following (the WebKit re-entry race). The rule cannot tell the inverse order apart: a downward movement that lands at the old end after a row measurement grew the extent below the reader (an image, a widget or an expanding tool, which refreshes no observation) resumes following up to that growth short of the real bottom, and the next content pins the reader to latest. The transcript change's own poke is always ordered, since its event comes after the meta's. | 2026-11-06 | MAWS e2e (the quit guard's 1 mid-turn, not asked, in e2e:supervisor-daemon) | Flagged by CI-opt past three in seven days, taken out of the hosted-Mac flake row's entry (12). Not a guard miscount: each case starts a turn and waits for its permission request before the quit, so one session is mid-turn, and on the daemon shape the guard asks about unsaved files alone, so it cannot refuse a quit for a session (it logs `not asked`). Two signatures. Case (h)'s line without its terminals clause (41358368598, 41550088326) is fixed on `fix/hosted-flakes-1106`: the case wrote a command to two shells and quit at once, before a shell had read its command, so the report counted whichever had started (old timing 9 of 10 `1 busy terminal`, fixed 10 of 10 `2 busy terminals`, probe run 42151919477); it now polls `pty.foreground` for both first. The fix did not hold under load: on 2026-11-09 (run 42710549674, the mini's overflow, a 15-minute load near 20, O8 M5b's tree) the case polled both shells busy and the guard's own reading at the quit still counted `1 busy terminal`, a third sighting in seven days, so the guard's reading at the quit and the test's poll can still disagree (P5 Decision Log 262). A fourth sighting on 2026-11-10 (run 42871682868, the mini's overflow at a 1-minute load of 10 to 15, O15's tree, whose count of busy terminals is master's unchanged): case (h) failed twice in the job, and the group alone passed (run 42899608186; P5 Decision Log 268). A fifth on 2026-11-13 (run 43520247669, the mini's host under `--lane no-hosted`, U3a's 4db11c30f, the guest busy beside it): the case polled both shells busy and the guard read `1 busy terminal`, 3.1 s into the case, so neither 8 s command had ended. The disagreement is C9's probe, not a stale guard: the guard asks `pty.foreground` live through `hosts.dispatch`, as the case's poll did, nothing cached (`src/main/app.ts:1032`), and the probe (`src/pty-host/foreground.ts:28`) reads a freshly spawned shell busy while node-pty's `spawn-helper` execs it (its foreground name is no shell's) and while a startup file runs a child, then idle until the shell reads the command it was given, then busy (node-pty 1.1.0 probed locally: busy, idle, busy within 20 ms for /bin/sh, across 700 ms for zsh with its startup files). The poll took the writer's spawn-time busy for its command, and the guard probed it in the idle stretch after. Fixed on `supervisor-settle-on-app-state` (ee48631a7): each command prints its start and holds the terminal as one job until the case releases it after the quit; the case waits for both starts in the app's ring, then for the guard's own report (`globalThis.__maws_quit`, under MAWS_E2E in an unpackaged build) to read what the quit's line is held to, and for both commands' end files with no app instead of a fixed 18 s. Not a product defect: on the daemon shape a quit asks only about unsaved files and logs the count, so no one is told a wrong number, and the probe's busy reading of a starting shell errs toward C9's close question (a tab closed in its first instant asks); the product is left as it is. Case (j)'s `MAWS did not quit` within 20 s after the guard's line (41995538110): the wait is named, below MAWS's code. Under a busy loop per core every close stalls past 30 s (51 of 51 in the PR #495 probes), and the stall comes after the app's own shutdown: `before-quit`, the window's `closed`, `will-quit` (held by MAWS's drains) and `quit 0` all fire within 0.3 s (probe run 42173010611; #495 read the absence of `will-quit` from main's stderr, but on macOS a write to a pipe is asynchronous and those lines were never flushed). Symbolicated against Electron 44.4.5's Breakpad symbols (probe run 42174440050), main waits in `content::BrowserMainLoop::ShutdownThreadsAndCleanUp` > `base::internal::TaskTracker::CompleteShutdown` for a BLOCK_SHUTDOWN task: `content::DeclarativePerformanceObserverStore::Backend::InitOnDbSequence` opening its SQLite database on a ThreadPoolBackgroundWorker, a background-QoS thread the kernel does not schedule while the cores are saturated (1860 of 1860 samples at `sqlite3MemInit + 0`; in the second sample the worker was still being started). Killing the profile's `mawsd` does not release it. The same class (a background SQLite init blocking shutdown, then BTM/DIPS once that store was disabled) is described in another Electron app's PR (hussainph/volli-code #748). (j)'s own red ended on Node's wait for the inspector, which came from `Scratch.quit` quitting while Playwright held it; `Scratch.quit` and the connector scene's quit now use Playwright's close, which detaches it. The mitigation is main's quit reaper (`src/main/lifecycle/reaper.ts`, `fix/quit-reaper`): at Electron's `quit`, after the shutdown list's steps and a new step that flushes every browser session's storage and cookies, a detached `/bin/sh` SIGKILLs main's Chromium helpers and then main 5 s later if main is still its parent (PR #502, P5 Decision Log 234), so a stalled quit ends within about 6 s instead of when the load drops; a MAWS-free Electron 44.4.5 app reproduces the stall (8 of 8 under one busy loop per core, none under two of three), and the upstream report is open as electron/electron#55647, filed 2026-11-06 on the owner's word. Owner: the MAWS architect. | a quit that lingers up to 5 s on a saturated Mac, then ends by SIGKILL | Electron ships a fix and the reaper's SIGKILL no longer fires under the busy-loop probe, or the reaper is retired by a ruling |

| 2026-11-10 | P5 O15, the document store's keystroke-to-durable-ack path (the MAWS architect) | The ack reads close to its bound, and most of what MAWS adds to the disk commit is not new code. On the mini the case reads p50 8.0 / p95 10.6 ms on the shape MAWS runs and 7.3 / 9.5 on the retired utility shape; the bound is 13 ms by the owner's ruling (O15 Decision Log 15), 10 ms before. The SQLite commit under `fullfsync` is p50 5.0 to 5.1 and p95 6.4 to 6.8 of that, which is what a bare commit on the same drive reads. The rest is about 2.5 ms a keystroke on the utility shape and about 3.2 ms through `mawsd` (means, stage-timed): the engine host's receipt of the frame 1.06 (the apply to the live document 0.67 of it; the document kind's hooks and its host replica at most 0.36 of it, the only code on this path newer than the bound), the renderer's port both ways about 0.6, the renderer's own edit and post 0.39, the wait for the flush 0.14, `store.stamp` 0.13 (it prepares its statement on every commit), the outbox and the ack 0.14, and on the daemon shape the relay through main and `mawsd` 0.85, of which 0.08 is `mawsd`'s own code and the rest four socket hops and main's loop. The case adds about 0.3 ms of its own: the page still holds the last cold trial's handle on the document, so the host sends that second replica every update before the ack and the page applies it first (the ack waited behind it in about three of four edits). How much the path grew since the bound was set is not established: S2 and P3-A M9 were read on another Mac, and no run of that tree exists on the mini. Two cheap gains were left out of F, about 0.4 ms at p50 together, both in the engine host: send each connection's ack before the other connections' frames in the flush, and prepare the stamp's statement once. First sync is also near its bound on `mawsd` (142.8 against 150 ms). | Runs 42837591610 (`mawsd`, valid, loads 3.02 to 2.41) and 42839107435 (utility, valid); the stage-timed runs 42853148260 and 42854726178 on diagnostic branches (each stage on `process.hrtime`, the stages summing to the measured mean; the instrumentation itself reads 0.2 to 0.4 ms slower at p50); a bare-commit bench on the mini the same day (p95 6.3 and 6.8 ms). The architect holds the per-edit series and the analyzer outside the repository. | The two gains are in or dismissed with numbers; the case closes its cold-trial handle before the ack window; and the case reads a bare commit in the same run and judges the ack against it, which changes how the bound is judged and so is the owner's to rule. The registry's app side (PR #571) read over the bound in all three valid readings of `e2e:daemon-idle-render` (43261189256: 100,339, 99,522 and 103,874 KiB), its own share the kept case's +2.2 MiB in `mawsd` (project-add garbage, left because `mawsd` never collects while clients other than the app are connected), and the render case's overage mostly the supervisor's drift. That work is in flight on branch `p5-idle-margin`; the placement's largest estimate is its cut 1, collecting on activity-quiet with clients connected (`src/mawsd/daemon.ts:528`, `src/shared/host/idle-collect.ts`; about 1.5 to 2.5 MiB in `mawsd`, up to about 4 MiB in the supervisor with the browser idle or sessions kept). 2026-11-13, the outcome (P5 Decision Log 273): cut 1 landed as the idle collection's quiet entry (merge 4109f5e25), and the one-session cases are back under the bound on valid readings, 98,675 and 98,179 KiB after a render host has ended and 97,523 with the browser idle (runs 43344708307 and 43350172010), `e2e:daemon-idle` at 95,843 (43342345313); the four-kept case still reads over (the wire registry's row of 2026-11-12). Forms known: a link while an image's `![` is still open before it; a link whose `[` precedes a literal URL in the same paragraph (a URL's extent is over-approximated); the rest of a paragraph after a resource whose destination or title runs past 16 KiB; a footnote's definition even where a call names it; a list item's own indentation and an ordered item that cannot interrupt a paragraph, read as code or as no new block; a fence closed only at its opening's column (a less indented one, in a list item, read as the same code); a table under a paragraph; a reference definition whose destination or title runs past 16 KiB, taken to its paragraph's end. Since the work meter (Decision Log 24), also whatever passes one of its caps: a text whose scan would pass 64 visits per character; a line with more than 32 container markers, or whose block-level test (a thematic break, a fence's info, a closing fence, a setext underline, a delimiter row, a table row's cells, its indentation or a blank run) cannot decide within 16 KiB (the whole text); more than 1,024 brackets open in one paragraph (the rest of the text); an autolink candidate (`<` and what follows up to its first `>`, space or `<`) past 16 KiB (the rest of its paragraph); a bracket text over 999 characters taken as a defined reference whenever any label is defined; a tool name over 1,024 characters taken as no MCP tool's. The fix round 1 differential prints a few more per run as window-only chips. | `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-12-ux-wave-U6c-linked-file.md`, fix round 1 (the branch review's items 1 and 2 and the differential); `src/renderer/src/tools/code/named-links.test.tsx`, "the differential: every path the rule names is a chip the window draws" (seed 0x6c696e6b, 2000 strings: 70 window-only chips, none named and not drawn; seeds 1 to 6 at 3000 strings pass; since the work meter 1,245 named paths and 71 window-only chips, the two names fewer being links after an autolink candidate 17 KiB long); `src/shared/files/named.test.ts`, "the scan’s work, bounded by construction (Decision Log 24)". | The rule is replaced: parse the pre-filtered text block with the window's own parser instead of extending the mirror; the differential then asserts equality. That exit removes the mirror from `src/shared/files/named.ts`; splitting the mirror into a module of its own before then is optional tidying. A stronger exit for the bound: running the search off the daemon's event loop with a hard stop at the deadline would make the bound independent of the scanner. |
